```
29	class DiscoveryService:
30	    """Provider-independent scholarly discovery workflows."""
31	
32	    def __init__(self, catalog: ProviderCatalog, default_sources: tuple[str, ...]) -> None:
33	        if not default_sources:
34	            raise ValueError("at least one default discovery source is required")
35	        self._catalog = catalog
36	        self._default_sources = default_sources
37	
38	    @property
39	    def providers(self) -> tuple[str, ...]:
40	        return self._catalog.names()
41	
42	    async def search(self, request: SearchRequest, *, source: str = "all") -> SearchResponse:
43	        if source.casefold() != "all":
44	            provider = cast(SearchProvider, self._catalog.get(source, Capability.SEARCH))
45	            page = await self._search_provider(provider, request)
46	            return SearchResponse(
47	                source=provider.name,
48	                providers=(self._report(page),),
49	                works=page.works,
50	            )
51	
52	        providers = [
53	            cast(SearchProvider, self._catalog.get(name, Capability.SEARCH))
54	            for name in self._default_sources
55	        ]
56	        outcomes = await asyncio.gather(
57	            *(self._search_provider(provider, request) for provider in providers),
58	            return_exceptions=True,
59	        )
60	        reports: list[ProviderReport] = []
61	        works: list[WorkRecord] = []
62	        for provider, outcome in zip(providers, outcomes, strict=True):
63	            if isinstance(outcome, asyncio.CancelledError):
64	                raise outcome
65	            if isinstance(outcome, Exception):
66	                reports.append(
67	                    ProviderReport(
68	                        provider=provider.name,
69	                        status="error",
70	                        error=str(outcome) or type(outcome).__name__,
71	                    )
72	                )
73	                continue
74	            reports.append(self._report(outcome))
75	            works.extend(outcome.works)
76	        return SearchResponse(
77	            source="all",
78	            providers=tuple(reports),
79	            works=merge_works(works),
80	        )
81	
82	    async def get_work(self, identifier: str, *, source: str = "openalex") -> WorkRecord:
83	        identifier = self._nonblank(identifier, label="identifier")
84	        provider = cast(WorkProvider, self._catalog.get(source, Capability.GET_WORK))
85	        return await provider.get_work(identifier)
86	
87	    async def related(
88	        self,
89	        identifier: str,
90	        *,
91	        kind: RelatedKind = "citations",
92	        source: str | None = None,
93	        limit: int = 25,
94	    ) -> RelatedResponse:
95	        identifier = self._nonblank(identifier, label="identifier")
96	        if not 1 <= limit <= 50:
97	            raise ValueError("related-work limit must be between 1 and 50")
98	        capability = Capability(kind)
99	        selected = source or ("semanticscholar" if kind == "recommendations" else "openalex")
100	        provider = cast(RelatedProvider, self._catalog.get(selected, capability))
101	        works = await provider.related(identifier, kind, limit)
102	        return RelatedResponse(provider=provider.name, kind=kind, works=works)
103	
104	    async def resolve(self, reference: str, *, source: str = "openalex") -> ResolveResponse:
105	        reference = self._nonblank(reference, label="reference")
106	        provider = cast(ResolveProvider, self._catalog.get(source, Capability.RESOLVE))
107	        works = await provider.resolve(reference)
108	        return ResolveResponse(provider=provider.name, reference=reference, works=works)
109

111	    def _report(page: SearchPage) -> ProviderReport:
112	        return ProviderReport(

114	            status="ok",
115	            total_available=page.total_available,
116	            returned=page.returned,
117	            next_start=page.next_start,
118	        )
119	
120	    @staticmethod
121	    async def _search_provider(
122	        provider: SearchProvider,
123	        request: SearchRequest,
124	    ) -> SearchPage:

126	        unsupported = [
127	            field
128	            for field in SEARCH_CONSTRAINT_FIELDS
129	            if getattr(request, field) not in (None, "", ()) and field not in supported
130	        ]
131	        if unsupported:
132	            raise ValueError(
133	                f"{provider.name} does not support search constraints: "
134	                + ", ".join(unsupported)
135	            )
136	        return await provider.search(request)
137	
138	    @staticmethod

140	        if not isinstance(value, str) or not value.strip():
141	            raise ValueError(f"{label} must be a nonblank string")
142	        return value.strip()
```

```
97	class _WorkerDownloadSink:
98	    """Write and hash one bounded stream on a dedicated filesystem worker."""
99	
100	    def __init__(
101	        self,
102	        destination: str,
103	        algorithms: tuple[str, ...],
104	        publication_root: PinnedPublicationRoot,
105	    ) -> None:
106	        self._destination = destination
107	        self._algorithms = algorithms
108	        self._publication_root = publication_root
109	        self._executor = ThreadPoolExecutor(
110	            max_workers=1,
111	            thread_name_prefix="procurement-download",
112	        )
113	        self._handle: BinaryIO | None = None
114	        self._digesters: dict[str, Any] = {}
115	        self._total = 0
116	        self._owns_destination = False
117	
118	    def _submit(self, function: Callable[..., _T], *args: object):
119	        loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
120	        return loop.run_in_executor(self._executor, partial(function, *args))
121	
122	    async def _run(self, function: Callable[..., _T], *args: object) -> _T:
123	        """Settle one worker operation before propagating any cancellation."""
124	
125	        future = self._submit(function, *args)
126	        cancelled = False
127	        while True:
128	            try:
129	                result = await asyncio.shield(future)
130	            except asyncio.CancelledError:
131	                cancelled = True
132	                if future.done():
133	                    try:
134	                        future.result()
135	                    except BaseException:
136	                        pass
137	                    raise
138	                continue
139	            except BaseException:
140	                if cancelled:
141	                    raise asyncio.CancelledError()
142	                raise
143	            if cancelled:
144	                raise asyncio.CancelledError()
145	            return result
146	
147	    def _open(self) -> None:
148	        self._handle = self._publication_root.open_file(self._destination, "xb")
149	        self._owns_destination = True
150	        self._digesters = {name: hashlib.new(name) for name in self._algorithms}
151	
152	    def _write(self, chunk: bytes) -> None:
153	        if self._handle is None:
154	            raise RuntimeError("download sink is not open")
155	        written = self._handle.write(chunk)
156	        if written != len(chunk):
157	            raise OSError(
158	                f"short artifact write ({written} of {len(chunk)} bytes): "
159	                f"'{self._destination}'"
160	            )
161	        for digester in self._digesters.values():
162	            digester.update(chunk)
163	        self._total += written
164	
165	    def _finish(self) -> tuple[int, tuple[tuple[str, str], ...]]:
166	        if self._handle is None:
167	            raise RuntimeError("download sink is not open")
168	        try:
169	            self._handle.flush()
170	            os.fsync(self._handle.fileno())
171	        finally:
172	            self._handle.close()
173	            self._handle = None
174	        return self._total, tuple(
175	            (name, self._digesters[name].hexdigest()) for name in self._algorithms
176	        )
177	
178	    def _abort(self) -> None:
179	        if self._handle is not None:
180	            try:
181	                self._handle.close()
182	            finally:
183	                self._handle = None
184	        if self._owns_destination:
185	            try:
186	                self._publication_root.unlink(self._destination)
187	            except FileNotFoundError:
188	                pass
189	
190	    async def open(self) -> None:
191	        await self._run(self._open)
192	
193	    async def write(self, chunk: bytes) -> None:
194	        await self._run(self._write, chunk)
195	
196	    async def finish(self) -> tuple[int, tuple[tuple[str, str], ...]]:
197	        return await self._run(self._finish)
198	
199	    async def abort(self) -> None:
200	        await self._run(self._abort)
201	
202	    def shutdown(self) -> None:
203	        """Release the idle worker after its file boundary has settled."""
204	
205	        self._executor.shutdown(wait=True, cancel_futures=True)
206

208	class RateLimiter:
209	    """Per-key monotonic request clocks shared by concurrent provider calls."""
210	
211	    def __init__(self, *, clock: Clock = time.monotonic, sleep: Sleeper = asyncio.sleep) -> None:
212	        self._clock = clock
213	        self._sleep = sleep
214	        self._last_request: dict[str, float] = {}
215	        self._locks: dict[str, asyncio.Lock] = {}
216	
217	    async def wait(self, key: str, interval_seconds: float) -> None:
218	        if interval_seconds <= 0:
219	            return
220	        lock = self._locks.setdefault(key, asyncio.Lock())
221	        async with lock:
222	            now = self._clock()
223	            last = self._last_request.get(key)
224	            if last is not None:
225	                remaining = interval_seconds - (now - last)
226	                if remaining > 0:
227	                    await self._sleep(remaining)
228	            self._last_request[key] = self._clock()
229	
230	
231	class HttpClient:
232	    """Owned HTTPX client with injected rate and retry state."""
233	
234	    def __init__(
235	        self,
236	        client: httpx.AsyncClient | None = None,
237	        *,
238	        rate_limiter: RateLimiter | None = None,
239	        sleep: Sleeper = asyncio.sleep,
240	        utc_now: UtcNow = lambda: datetime.now(timezone.utc),
241	    ) -> None:
242	        self._client = client or httpx.AsyncClient(follow_redirects=True)
243	        self._owns_client = client is None
244	        self._limiter = rate_limiter or RateLimiter(sleep=sleep)
245	        self._sleep = sleep
246	        self._utc_now = utc_now
247	
248	    async def close(self) -> None:
249	        if self._owns_client:
250	            await self._client.aclose()
251	
252	    async def get(
253	        self,
254	        url: str,
255	        *,
256	        params: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None,
257	        headers: Mapping[str, str] | None = None,
258	        rate_key: str | None = None,
259	        policy: RequestPolicy = RequestPolicy(),
260	    ) -> httpx.Response:
261	        key = rate_key or httpx.URL(url).host
262	        last_error: Exception | None = None
263	        for attempt in range(1, policy.max_attempts + 1):
264	            await self._limiter.wait(key, policy.min_interval_seconds)
265	            try:
266	                request = self._client.build_request(
267	                    "GET",
268	                    url,
269	                    params=params,
270	                    headers=headers,
271	                    timeout=policy.timeout_seconds,
272	                )
273	                response = await self._client.send(request, stream=True)
274	            except httpx.TransportError as exc:
275	                last_error = exc
276	                if attempt == policy.max_attempts:
277	                    raise ProviderHttpError(
278	                        f"network request failed for {key}: {type(exc).__name__}"
279	                    ) from exc
280	            else:
281	                if 200 <= response.status_code < 300:
282	                    return await self._bounded_response(response, policy)
283	                await response.aclose()
284	                message = self._response_message(response)
285	                if response.status_code in policy.rate_limit_statuses:
286	                    if not policy.retry_rate_limits or attempt == policy.max_attempts:
287	                        raise ProviderRateLimitError(message, status_code=response.status_code)
288	                    delay = self._retry_after_seconds(
289	                        response,
290	                        default=policy.backoff_seconds * (2 ** (attempt - 1)),
291	                    )
292	                    if delay > policy.max_retry_after_seconds:
293	                        raise ProviderRateLimitError(message, status_code=response.status_code)
294	                    last_error = ProviderRateLimitError(
295	                        message,
296	                        status_code=response.status_code,
297	                    )
298	                    await self._sleep(delay)
299	                    continue
300	                if response.status_code not in policy.retry_statuses or attempt == policy.max_attempts:
301	                    raise ProviderHttpError(message, status_code=response.status_code)
302	                last_error = ProviderHttpError(message, status_code=response.status_code)
303	                delay = policy.backoff_seconds * (2 ** (attempt - 1))
304	                if response.status_code == 503:
305	                    delay = self._retry_after_seconds(response, default=delay)
306	                if delay > policy.max_retry_after_seconds:
307	                    raise ProviderHttpError(message, status_code=response.status_code)
308	                await self._sleep(delay)
309	                continue
310	            await self._sleep(policy.backoff_seconds * (2 ** (attempt - 1)))
311	        raise ProviderHttpError(f"request attempts exhausted for {key}: {last_error}")
312	
313	    async def _bounded_response(
314	        self,
315	        response: httpx.Response,
316	        policy: RequestPolicy,
317	    ) -> httpx.Response:
318	        """Buffer at most the configured HTTP-decoded entity bytes."""
319	
320	        content_length = response.headers.get("content-length")
321	        if content_length is not None:
322	            try:
323	                declared_length = int(content_length)
324	            except ValueError:
325	                declared_length = None
326	            if (
327	                declared_length is not None

329	            ):
330	                await response.aclose()

332	                    "provider response exceeds the decoded-body limit for "
333	                    f"{self._evidence_url(response.url)}"
334	                )
335	
336	        chunks: list[bytes] = []
337	        total = 0
338	        try:
339	            async for chunk in response.aiter_bytes():
340	                total += len(chunk)
```

```
603	            except (TypeError, ValueError, OverflowError):

607	            now = self._utc_now()
608	            if now.tzinfo is None:
609	                now = now.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)
610	            return max(0.0, (retry_at - now).total_seconds())
```

```
43	def _open_payload(
44	    path: str | Path,
45	    publication_root: PinnedPublicationRoot | None,
46	) -> BinaryIO:
47	    if publication_root is not None:
48	        return publication_root.open_file(path, "rb")
49	    return Path(path).open("rb")
50	
51	
52	def validate_gzip_payload(
53	    path: str | Path,
54	    *,
55	    maximum_expanded_bytes: int,
56	    publication_root: PinnedPublicationRoot | None = None,
57	) -> None:
58	    """Validate one complete non-empty gzip stream within its expansion boundary."""
59	
60	    expanded = 0
61	    try:
62	        with _open_payload(path, publication_root) as handle:
63	            with gzip.GzipFile(fileobj=handle, mode="rb") as stream:
64	                while chunk := stream.read(1024 * 1024):
65	                    expanded += len(chunk)
66	                    if expanded > maximum_expanded_bytes:
67	                        raise AcquisitionError(
68	                            "gzip payload exceeds the configured expanded-byte limit"
69	                        )
70	    except AcquisitionError:
71	        raise
72	    except (OSError, EOFError) as exc:
73	        raise AcquisitionError("gzip payload is incomplete or invalid") from exc
74	    if expanded == 0:
75	        raise AcquisitionError("gzip payload expands to an empty body")
76	
77	
78	def validate_pdf_payload(
79	    path: str | Path,
80	    *,
81	    publication_root: PinnedPublicationRoot | None = None,
82	) -> None:
83	    """Validate the required leading and trailing markers of one PDF payload."""
84	
85	    with _open_payload(path, publication_root) as handle:
86	        size = os.fstat(handle.fileno()).st_size
87	        prefix = handle.read(5)
88	        handle.seek(max(0, size - 4096))
89	        suffix = handle.read()
90	    if prefix != b"%PDF-" or b"%%EOF" not in suffix:
91	        raise AcquisitionError("PDF payload lacks its required header or end marker")

94	class _AsyncAcquisitionTransaction:
95	    """Run one thread-local staging lease and its filesystem work off the event loop."""
96	
97	    def __init__(self, store: AcquisitionStore, slug: str) -> None:
98	        self._context = store.transaction(slug)
99	        self._executor = ThreadPoolExecutor(
100	            max_workers=1,
101	            thread_name_prefix="procurement-acquisition",
102	        )
103	        self.item: AcquisitionItem | None = None
104	
105	    def _submit(self, function: Callable[..., _T], *args: object, **kwargs: object):
106	        loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
107	        return loop.run_in_executor(self._executor, partial(function, *args, **kwargs))
108	
109	    async def run(
110	        self,
111	        function: Callable[..., _T],
112	        *args: object,
113	        **kwargs: object,
114	    ) -> _T:
115	        """Finish an in-flight filesystem boundary before propagating cancellation."""
116	
117	        future = self._submit(function, *args, **kwargs)
118	        try:
119	            return await asyncio.shield(future)
120	        except asyncio.CancelledError:
121	            try:
122	                await future
123	            except BaseException:
124	                pass
125	            raise
126	
127	    async def __aenter__(self) -> "_AsyncAcquisitionTransaction":
128	        future = self._submit(self._context.__enter__)
129	        try:
130	            self.item = await asyncio.shield(future)
131	        except asyncio.CancelledError:
132	            try:
133	                self.item = await future
134	            except BaseException:
135	                pass
136	            else:
137	                try:
138	                    await self.run(self._context.__exit__, None, None, None)
139	                finally:
140	                    self.item = None
141	            self._executor.shutdown(wait=True)
142	            raise
143	        except BaseException:
144	            self._executor.shutdown(wait=True)
145	            raise
146	        return self
147	
148	    async def __aexit__(
149	        self,
150	        exc_type: type[BaseException] | None,
151	        exc: BaseException | None,
152	        traceback: TracebackType | None,
153	    ) -> bool:
154	        try:
155	            return bool(await self.run(self._context.__exit__, exc_type, exc, traceback))
156	        finally:
157	            self.item = None
158	            self._executor.shutdown(wait=True)

183	    async def plan(self, request: ArtifactAcquisitionRequest) -> ArtifactPlan:
184	        """Return an internal server-produced plan; callers cannot supply candidate URLs."""
185	
186	        provider = self._catalog.get(request.provider, Capability.PLAN_ARTIFACT)
187	        plan = await provider.plan_artifact(request)
188	        if plan.artifact.provider.casefold() != provider.name.casefold():
189	            raise AcquisitionError("artifact planner returned another provider's identity")
190	        if set(plan.requested) != set(request.artifacts):
191	            raise AcquisitionError("artifact planner did not account for the request")
192	        return plan
```

> **[thinking]**
>
> I'm reviewing the security implementation for the download handler—it uses a manual redirect loop with `follow_redirects=False` to enforce strict validation at each hop, including HTTPS-to-HTTP downgrade prevention, URL safety checks against an allowed hosts list, and a redirect cap. The handler also implements streaming protections like mid-stream byte limits with backpressure, fsync before completion, and validation that declared content length matches actual bytes written.
>
> I'm noting that the `get()` API method follows redirects at the client level, which means it could theoretically downgrade from HTTPS to HTTP unlike the artifact downloads—the README's security claim about non-loopback routes requiring HTTPS is scoped to downloads but could be clearer about whether this applies to API metadata reads too. The severity is low since provider base URLs are HTTPS and these are just metadata operations. The `is_safe_artifact_url` check validates scheme and credentials per hop against pinned allowed hosts, though it doesn't restrict ports. The initial URL is checked on the first loop iteration, so that's covered.
>
> For the acquire transaction, I'm seeing the full flow: validate the existing form, check for conflicts with unreceipted targets, download to a private path with validation of size, SHA, checksums, and payload sniffing, then journal→publish→collate→publish manifest→delete journal using a write-ahead pattern. The cancellation handling in `_AsyncAcquisitionTransaction.__aenter__` is careful about cleanup. HTML validation is minimal with just a marker check. The design wraps candidate errors into `AcquisitionOutcome(status="error")` so partial failures are explicit. Gzip validation streams to a cap and PDF checks markers at head and tail. The architecture doc headings align with what's actually delivered—jobs aren't provider globals, acquisition is transactional, migration sequence is clear, article publication is the invariant.
>
> I don't need to deep-dive into storage/acquisitions.py, source_deposits.py, the source modules, materialization, local_import, metadata, or the provider-specific code—those are covered by the architectural overview I have. Idempotence *and* conflict *and* receipt-bytes-unchanged *and* no partial residue. Complemented by `:153` (`test_invalid
```
_kind_truncated_payload_and_kind_limits_publish_nothing`), which drives three refusal classes and then asserts globally:
```
165 assert not list(staging.rglob("acquisition.json"))
166 assert not list(staging.rglob(".download.part"))
```
and by `:212` (`test_detected_source_mutation_cleans_private_copy`), which monkeypatches `_same_open_snapshot` to `False
` and asserts the same two absences at `:223ΓÇô224`.

Verdict: mechanism-level, with one caveat ΓÇö the *physical* confinement half of the same behavior (`test_local_import.p
y:169`) has its symlink cases silently skipped here (A2 ┬ºc), so only the directory case (`:179ΓÇô181`) and the request-
validation cases (`:175ΓÇô177`) actually execute.

---

## Questions for the primary

1. **Is the silent-fallback pattern intentional?** `test_acquisition.py:842ΓÇô847`, `test_local_import.py:317ΓÇô322`, an
d `test_local_import.py:186ΓÇô199` all degrade to a happy-path assertion on Windows without emitting a skip, while `test
_materialization.py:510` handles the identical situation by asserting the Windows outcome. If the materialization form i
s the intended house style, three tests need conversion; if not, I'd want to know why the acquisition lane differs. This
 is my highest-value finding and it is a policy call, not a fact.

2. **`test_acquisition.py:755` ΓÇö does it run or escape here?** My reading is that the item directory is not yet pinned
 when the mocked `pin_child` renames it, so `swapped` should be `True` on Windows and the real assertion should fire. I
could not settle this read-only. A one-line probe would.

3. **Is `procurement/source/_safety.py`'s reparse guard meant to be witnessed?** `jsonl_engine`'s `test_inventory_catalo
g.py:48` proves the junction technique works without privilege on this host. Extending it to `_safety.py:75,94` and the
five unwitnessed `jsonl_engine` reparse checks looks cheap, but it is a scope decision.

4. **Are the six never-invoked MCP tools deliberately registration-only?** `plan_artifact_acquisition` / `acquire_artifa
ct` / `get_acquisition_receipt` marshal arguments (`tools/acquisition.py:38,58`) that no test exercises through the prot
ocol. If the capability-layer tests are considered sufficient, that's a defensible line ΓÇö but it should be a stated on
e, since `discover_related`, `resolve_reference`, and `get_work` are in the same position.

5. **Was `download_to`'s guard set (`http.py:408ΓÇô422`) meant to be contract-tested, or is it defense-in-depth behind `
composition.py`?** The empty-`allowed_hosts` case in particular disables host confinement outright, which reads like som
ething worth pinning regardless.

6. **Does the compressed-and-truncated download case matter?** `http.py:503` skips the declared-length truncation check
whenever `content-encoding` is present. I could not tell read-only whether providers in scope ever serve `content-encodi
ng` on artifact routes.

7. **`tests/jsonl_engine/` was in scope for witnessing procurement behaviors, but I did not audit its 152 tests on their
 own terms** ΓÇö I read only the seven files reachable from the skip list and the materialization path. Say the word if
the primary wants that tree censused too.
```

```
# Lane A ΓÇö procurement redevelopment review: tests & coverage

Scope read: 54 modules under `src/procurement/**` (10,566 lines) and 12 under `src/mcp-servers/procurement_mcp/**` (485 lines); 18 test files under `tests/procurement/`, 2 under `tests/mcp-servers/procurement_mcp/`, plus the `tests/jsonl_engine/` witnesses reached by materialization. Census cross-checked against `scratch/procurement-review/pytest-collect.txt` (439 collected) and `pytest-main.txt` (429 passed / 10 skipped / 140 subtests).

### Uncovered / thin spots, ranked by unwitnessed risk

**1. `transport/http.py` `download_to` pre-flight guards ΓÇö all five untested (CONFIRMED).**
No test anywhere asserts the messages at `src/procurement/transport/http.py:409` (`download_to requires an active PinnedPublicationRoot`), `:414` (`requires at least one allowed host`), `:416` (`private download path already exists`), or `:422` (`unsupported download hash algorithm`). Verified by grepping those literals across `tests/` ΓÇö the only hits are unrelated PowerShell suites and `test_source_extraction.py:408`. Risk: an empty `allowed_hosts` tuple would silently disable host confinement, and nothing pins that it is refused.

**2. Redirect-loop exhaustion and missing `Location` ΓÇö untested (CONFIRMED).**
`http.py:462ΓÇô470` raises for an omitted `Location` and for exceeding `max_redirects`. Grepping `redirect|Location|max_redirects` across `tests/` yields only the three redirect tests at `test_acquisition.py:585, 618, 642`, none of which chains more than one hop. Risk: an unbounded or malformed redirect chain is a confinement bypass with no witness.

**3. `RequestPolicy.__post_init__` validation ΓÇö untested (CONFIRMED).** `http.py:86ΓÇô94` rejects negative intervals, `max_attempts < 1`, negative backoff, and `max_decoded_body_bytes < 1`. No test constructs an invalid policy. Risk: a nonsensical policy silently disables retry or capping.

**4. `procurement_mcp/tools/*` ΓÇö 6 of 16 tools never invoked (CONFIRMED).**
`test_registration.py:19ΓÇô151` pins name, description, and input/output schema fingerprints for all 16, but `call_tool(...)` across both MCP test files reaches only `discover_search`, `prepare_source_deposit_metadata`, `prepare_article_metadata_by_doi`, `list_local_import_inboxes`, `import_local_artifact`, `materialize_source_deposit`, `list_article_catalogs`, `inspect_article_catalog`, `rebuild_article_inventory`, `list_procurement_providers`. Never executed through the protocol: **`discover_related`, `resolve_reference`, `get_work`, `plan_artifact_acquisition`, `acquire_artifact`, `get_acquisition_receipt`**. Risk: the three acquisition tools' argument marshalling (`tools/acquisition.py:38,58` ΓÇö the `artifacts=("source",)` default and the `tuple(artifacts)` coercion) is unwitnessed end-to-end, and errors would surface only at runtime.

**5. Every tool's "service is not configured" branch ΓÇö untested (CONFIRMED).** `tools/acquisition.py:33,53,71,82,96` and siblings raise `RuntimeError` when the application lacks a service. `RecordingApplication` (`test_registration.py:187`) supplies only `local_import`, but no test calls a tool whose service is absent. Risk: a partially-composed application returns an unhelpful protocol error shape nobody has seen.

**6. `runtime.finish_sync` cancellation branch ΓÇö untested (CONFIRMED).** `runtime.py:17ΓÇô22` settles the worker thread before re-raising `CancelledError`. No MCP test cancels a tool call. Risk: this is exactly the "reach the synchronous boundary before propagating cancellation" guarantee the capability layer tests hard (`test_acquisition.py:916`, `test_local_import.py:227`), and the MCP re-implementation of it has no witness.

**7. `source/_safety.py` has no direct test file (CONFIRMED).** `_portable_leaf` / `_portable_relative` / `_PortablePathRegistry` are well-exercised through `test_source_extraction.py:289,336`. But `_plain_directory` (`:69`) and `_regular_file` (`:88`) ΓÇö the reparse-point and symlink refusals ΓÇö are reached only through `local_import`, where the test that would drive them is silently skipped on Windows (see A2 ┬º3). Risk: the reparse guard at `_safety.py:75,94` is the module's Windows-specific defense and has zero Windows execution.

**8. `storage/source_deposits.py` (565 lines) is the largest via-service-only module (CONFIRMED).** Only `test_materialization.py:131` constructs it. Its internals (lease, private-tree sweep, generation checks) are asserted through materialization outcomes at `test_materialization.py:461,478,526` rather than at its own API. Risk: a behavior change inside the store that materialization happens not to observe passes green.

**9. `source/tree.py` (290 lines) has one dedicated test (CONFIRMED).** `test_source_tree.py` collects exactly one test ΓÇö `test_tree_fingerprint_is_sorted_and_deterministic` ΓÇö cross-checking against `jsonl_engine.deposit._fingerprint_tree`. Risk: fingerprint agreement is pinned; traversal edge cases (empty dirs, deep nesting, non-regular entries) are not.

**10. `configuration/loader.py` error path untested (INFERRED).** `test_composition.py:69` calls `load_settings()` with no argument; nothing passes an explicit path or an unreadable/invalid file, so `ConfigurationError` at `loader.py:24` is unwitnessed. Low risk ΓÇö one `except` clause.

**11. `domain/deposits.py::validate_deposit_slug` has no direct test (CONFIRMED).** It is the slug gate for `AcquisitionStore.transaction` (`storage/acquisitions.py:388`), `artifact_path` (`:271`), and `AcquisitionItem.__init__` (`:206`). Only `PORTABLE_LEAF_PATTERN` is imported directly (`test_server.py:32`). Risk: rejection classes are asserted only through the paths that happen to call it.

**12. `procurement_mcp/__main__.py` untested.** Six lines, stdio entrypoint. Negligible.

---

## A2 ΓÇö Skip audit

Ten skips, all in `tests/jsonl_engine/**` except one. They fall into two distinct classes, and the class matters more than the count.

### Class 1 ΓÇö Symlink-privilege skips (6 of 10). Runtime `skipTest`, not a platform pairing.

| Reported at | Guard | Invariant unverified |
|---|---|---|
| `test_deposit.py:1013` | `skipTest` at `:1022` | article-route deposit refuses a symlinked `article.json` target |
| `test_deposit.py:1083` | `skipTest` at `:1093` | deposit refuses a symlinked provider-metadata target |
| `test_documents.py:101` | `skipTest` at `:111` | `pin_child` rejects a redirecting directory leaf |
| `test_inventory_catalog.py:510` | `skipTest` at `:517` | inventory refuses a `symbolic link or reparse point` entry |
| `test_publication.py:54` | `skipTest` at `:66` | nested access rejects a redirecting directory component |
| `test_publication.py:346` | `skipTest` at `:357` | publication refuses a `link, reparse point` destination |

**Does a Windows-alternate witness exist? Partially ΓÇö CONFIRMED.** `tests/jsonl_engine/test_inventory_catalog.py:48ΓÇô62` defines `_create_directory_junction` using `mklink /J`, which needs no privilege, and drives it at `:82` (`test_pinned_root_rejects_a_unicode_casefold_ancestor_junction`) and `:101` (`test_precheck_to_pin_ancestor_junction_swap_is_refused`). Those did **not** appear in the skip list, so they ran here. So the reparse-point defense **is** witnessed on Windows ΓÇö but only for the *pinned-root ancestor* path in `inventory_catalog`.

The gap: the junction technique is used in exactly one file. The five other reparse/symlink refusals above ΓÇö `deposit`, `documents.pin_child`, `publication`'s nested-component and destination checks, and `inventory_catalog`'s per-entry check at `:510` ΓÇö have **no** junction-based counterpart and are therefore entirely unwitnessed on this host, despite reparse points being a Windows-native attack surface rather than a POSIX one. `procurement/source/_safety.py:75,94` is in the same position with no witness at all.

### Class 2 ΓÇö Explicit `skipIf(os.name == "nt")` (4 of 10). Pairing must be checked individually.

I enumerated every `skipUnless(os.name == "nt")` in the tree; there are exactly two: `test_documents.py:130` and `test_concurrency.py:148`. That bounds the pairing question.

| Skip | Reason text | Windows-alternate witness? |
|---|---|---|
| `test_documents.py:117` ΓÇö `test_current_path_check_detects_a_replaced_posix_root` | "Windows holds the named route against replacement" | **YES ΓÇö genuinely paired.** `test_documents.py:130` `@skipUnless(os.name == "nt")` `test_windows_pin_blocks_root_replacement_until_close` asserts `PermissionError` on `os.rename` at `:139ΓÇô140`, then that the rename succeeds after `__exit__` at `:143ΓÇô144`. This is the model pairing. |
| `test_documents.py:198` ΓÇö `test_store_refuses_to_write_after_the_named_root_is_replaced` | "POSIX route replacement witness" | **NO paired test.** Defensible: the precondition (a replaced root) is unreachable on Windows *because* `:130` holds. But nothing states that dependency, and the skip reason is a label, not an explanation. |
| `test_publication.py:382` ΓÇö `test_article_refuses_a_replacement_generation` | "Windows retains the article route against replacement" | **NO paired test.** Same logic as above; the claim in the reason string is asserted only at the root level in `test_documents.py:130`, not for the article route. |
| `tests/procurement/test_catalog_service.py:86` ΓÇö `test_service_refuses_a_replacement_of_the_configured_catalog_generation` | "Windows retained roots block route replacement" | **NO paired test.** This is the one procurement-owned skip. `ArticleCatalogService.inspect`/`rebuild` refusing `InventoryCatalogError, "retained generation"` (`:96ΓÇô99`) has no Windows execution and no Windows-side assertion that the replacement is impossible. |

**Mechanism backing the "Windows blocks it" claims (CONFIRMED).** `src/jsonl_engine/publication.py:445` opens each pinned directory with share mode `0x0001 | 0x0002` and the inline comment *"READ | WRITE sharing; deliberately no DELETE sharing"*. That is why `os.rename` on a pinned directory raises `PermissionError` on Windows. The three unpaired skips are therefore *probably* moot on this platform ΓÇö but "probably moot" is an inference the suite never states.

### The larger A2 finding: silently self-disabling tests that emit no skip at all

Four tests take a `try: os.rename(...) except OSError: pass` fallback and then assert the **happy path** instead of the invariant. They report as passed, not skipped, so they are invisible in the 10-skip census ΓÇö the suite looks better covered than it is.

**(a) `tests/procurement/test_acquisition.py:802` `test_streamed_download_cannot_be_redirected_to_a_replacement_generation` ΓÇö CONFIRMED inert on Windows.**
```
842 try:
843 os.rename(target, displaced)
844 except OSError:
845 # Windows retains the configured and item directories without delete
846 # sharing. POSIX may rename them, but all file I/O remains descriptor-relative.
847 pass
...
854 if swapped:
855 with self.assertRaisesRegex(
856 AcquisitionConflictError,
857 "retained directory generation",
858 ):
...
861 else:
862 result = await asyncio.wait_for(task, timeout=2)
863 self.assertEqual(result.outcomes[0].status, "acquired")
```
Both subtests target directories held by an active pin ΓÇö `staging` (the store's root) and `staging/2008.10579v1` (pinned via `root.pin_child(slug)` at `storage/acquisitions.py:418`, live for the whole download). With no DELETE sharing (`publication.py:445`) the rename raises, `swapped` stays `False`, and the test asserts only `status == "acquired"`. The named invariant is never exercised here.

**(b) `tests/procurement/test_local_import.py:281` `test_local_copy_cannot_be_redirected_to_replaced_inbox_or_item` ΓÇö same shape, `:317ΓÇô322`**, with the same `else: assert result.outcomes[0].status == "acquired"` at `:344ΓÇô345`. CONFIRMED inert for the `item` case; INFERRED inert for `inbox` (the inbox root is opened by `ProcurementRootCatalog.open` at `storage/roots.py:156` and retained for the application lifetime, so it too is pinned).

**(c) `tests/procurement/test_local_import.py:186ΓÇô192` and `:195ΓÇô199`** ΓÇö `linked.symlink_to(...)` wrapped in `except OSError: pass`. On this host (no symlink privilege) both the symlinked-file and symlinked-inbox confinement assertions are skipped without a skip marker. These are the only tests that would drive `_safety.py`'s reparse/symlink refusals.

**(d) `tests/procurement/test_acquisition.py:755` `test_item_replacement_between_creation_and_pin_is_never_accepted`** ΓÇö same `try/except OSError` shape at `:772ΓÇô778` with a `blocked` fallback at `:798ΓÇô800`. **UNCERTAIN** whether it is inert here: the rename fires inside the mocked `pin_child` *before* `original_pin_child` runs, so the item directory is freshly `mkdir`'d and not yet pinned, and its parent's handle should not block renaming a child. I believe this one does exercise the real assertion on Windows, but I could not settle it read-only.

**The contrast that makes this a defect rather than a doctrine:** `tests/procurement/test_materialization.py:510ΓÇô515` and `:558ΓÇô563` face the identical situation and handle it correctly ΓÇö they branch on `os.name == "nt"` and **assert the Windows outcome**:
```
510 if os.name == "nt":
511 result = asyncio.run(materializer.materialize(request()))
512 assert result.status == "deposited"
513 assert replacement.blocked is True
514 assert replacement.swapped is False
515 assert not (layout.catalog_root / "retired-document").exists()
```
That pins "the replacement was refused by the OS" as a positive claim. The four tests above pin nothing.

---

## A3 ΓÇö Test-quality spot-check (5 behaviors)

### 1. `article.json` is published last ΓÇö **strong; asserts the mechanism.**
Strongest test: `tests/procurement/test_materialization.py:404` `test_interrupted_article_last_publication_recovers_immutable_components`. It injects a failure into the article-publication step only (`:414ΓÇô418`), then asserts the components exist and the sentinel does not:
```
437 assert archive.is_file()
438 assert metadata_path.is_file()
439 assert tree_main.is_file()
440 assert not (document / "article.json").exists()
441 assert not [name for name in os.listdir(document) if name.endswith(".tmp")]
```
and on re-run, that the already-published components are **byte- and mtime-identical**, not rewritten:
```
452 assert article_publication.calls == 2
453 for path, (raw, modified) in frozen.items():
454 assert path.read_bytes() == raw
455 assert path.stat().st_mtime_ns == modified
457 assert article["validation"]["publication"] == "recovered-existing-tree"
```
Verdict: this asserts ordering *and* the immutability that makes the ordering meaningful. `mtime_ns` equality is the decisive line ΓÇö a rewrite-with-same-bytes would pass a content check and fail this one. Reinforced at `test_materialization.py:368` (byte-idempotent article) and `:859`.

### 2. Destination no-replace ΓÇö **strong; asserts refusal plus non-mutation.**
Strongest test: `test_materialization.py:832` `test_conflicting_destination_archive_or_tree_is_never_replaced`, parametrized over `("archive", "tree")`:
```
851 before = conflict.read_bytes() if conflict.is_file() else (conflict / "main.tex").read_bytes()
852 with pytest.raises(SourceMaterializationError, match="conflict"):
...
857 after = conflict.read_bytes() if conflict.is_file() else (conflict / "main.tex").read_bytes()
858 assert after == before
859 assert not (document / "article.json").exists()
```
Verdict: refusal *and* byte-preservation of the occupant *and* absence of the sentinel. Not a happy-path surface. The receipt-level counterpart is equally strong ΓÇö `test_local_import.py:147ΓÇô150` asserts `acquisition.json` bytes are unchanged after a conflicting re-import, and `storage/acquisitions.py:298ΓÇô305` is the mechanism (`refusing to overwrite staged artifact` / `publish(..., overwrite=False)`).

### 3. Generation pinning across a transaction ΓÇö **the weakest of the five on this platform.**
Strongest test *by intent*: `test_acquisition.py:755` `test_item_replacement_between_creation_and_pin_is_never_accepted`:
```
794 self.assertIsNotNone(caught)
796 self.assertIn("changed while its generation was pinned", str(caught))
797 self.assertEqual(list((staging / "paper").iterdir()), [])
```
That is a real mechanism assertion ΓÇö the `_same_directory_generation` check at `storage/acquisitions.py:420ΓÇô423` ΓÇö and it also asserts no residue in the replacement directory. But it is gated behind `if swapped:` with an `else: self.assertTrue(blocked)` escape at `:798ΓÇô800`, and I could not confirm read-only which branch runs here (see A2 ┬ºd).

Its sibling `test_acquisition.py:802` is CONFIRMED to take the escape branch on Windows, where the only surviving assertion is `self.assertEqual(result.outcomes[0].status, "acquired")` (`:863`) ΓÇö a happy-path surface, not the named invariant. The genuinely-strong pinning witnesses that *do* execute here are `test_materialization.py:510ΓÇô515` and `:558ΓÇô563`, which assert `blocked is True` and that no retired directory was created.

Verdict: the mechanism is asserted where it runs, but the two acquisition-lane tests carrying the strongest phrasing are the two least likely to have run.

### 4. Bounded and hash-checked download ΓÇö **strong, with one real gap.**
Bounding is asserted at three independent points. Mid-stream cap, before any bytes are consumed:
```
test_http.py:317 with self.assertRaisesRegex(ProviderPayloadError, "decoded-body limit"):
test_http.py:318 asyncio.run(exercise())
test_http.py:319 self.assertFalse(UnreadBody.iterated)
```
ΓÇö `assertFalse(UnreadBody.iterated)` is the decisive line: it proves the declared-length rejection at `http.py:326ΓÇô334` fires *before* the body stream is touched. Post-decode bounding (so a gzip bomb is measured decoded, not compressed) at `test_http.py:274ΓÇô293`. Worker-write chunking at `test_http.py:80ΓÇô81` (`assertLessEqual(max(write_sizes), _DOWNLOAD_CHUNK_BYTES)`), which pins the `http.py:524` comment as behavior.

Hashing at `test_acquisition.py:610ΓÇô611`:
```
610 self.assertEqual(result.sha256, hashlib.sha256(PDF).hexdigest())
611 self.assertEqual(dict(result.digests)["md5"], hashlib.md5(PDF).hexdigest())
```
ΓÇö hashes the *decoded* bytes across a redirect hop, and `:612` asserts the evidence URL is credential-redacted. No-residue on refusal at `test_acquisition.py:700` (`assertFalse(os.path.lexists(destination))`), covering both oversize and truncated.

Verdict: mechanism-level. **Gap:** the truncation check compares against `declared_length` (`http.py:532ΓÇô535`), which is skipped entirely when `content-encoding` is present (`http.py:503`). No test covers a compressed, truncated artifact download ΓÇö the `truncated` case at `test_acquisition.py:681ΓÇô686` sends no `content-encoding`.

### 5. Local-import byte validation ΓÇö **strong; asserts refusal, residue, and non-mutation.**
Strongest test: `test_local_import.py:134` `test_same_bytes_are_idempotent_and_changed_bytes_conflict`:
```
142 assert first.outcomes[0].status == "acquired"
143 assert second.outcomes[0].status == "already-present"
147 with pytest.raises(AcquisitionConflictError, match="bytes conflict"):
149 assert (staging / "manual-paper" / "acquisition.json").read_bytes() == before
150 assert not (staging / "manual-paper" / ".download.part").exists()
```
Idempotence *and* conflict *and* receipt-bytes-unchanged *and* no partial residue. Complemented by `:153` (`test_invalid_kind_truncated_payload_and_kind_limits_publish_nothing`), which drives three refusal classes and then asserts globally:
```
165 assert not list(staging.rglob("acquisition.json"))
166 assert not list(staging.rglob(".download.part"))
```
and by `:212` (`test_detected_source_mutation_cleans_private_copy`), which monkeypatches `_same_open_snapshot` to `False` and asserts the same two absences at `:223ΓÇô224`.

Verdict: mechanism-level, with one caveat ΓÇö the *physical* confinement half of the same behavior (`test_local_import.py:169`) has its symlink cases silently skipped here (A2 ┬ºc), so only the directory case (`:179ΓÇô181`) and the request-validation cases (`:175ΓÇô177`) actually execute.

---

## Questions for the primary

1. **Is the silent-fallback pattern intentional?** `test_acquisition.py:842ΓÇô847`, `test_local_import.py:317ΓÇô322`, and `test_local_import.py:186ΓÇô199` all degrade to a happy-path assertion on Windows without emitting a skip, while `test_materialization.py:510` handles the identical situation by asserting the Windows outcome. If the materialization form is the intended house style, three tests need conversion; if not, I'd want to know why the acquisition lane differs. This is my highest-value finding and it is a policy call, not a fact.

2. **`test_acquisition.py:755` ΓÇö does it run or escape here?** My reading is that the item directory is not yet pinned when the mocked `pin_child` renames it, so `swapped` should be `True` on Windows and the real assertion should fire. I could not settle this read-only. A one-line probe would.

3. **Is `procurement/source/_safety.py`'s reparse guard meant to be witnessed?** `jsonl_engine`'s `test_inventory_catalog.py:48` proves the junction technique works without privilege on this host. Extending it to `_safety.py:75,94` and the five unwitnessed `jsonl_engine` reparse checks looks cheap, but it is a scope decision.

4. **Are the six never-invoked MCP tools deliberately registration-only?** `plan_artifact_acquisition` / `acquire_artifact` / `get_acquisition_receipt` marshal arguments (`tools/acquisition.py:38,58`) that no test exercises through the protocol. If the capability-layer tests are considered sufficient, that's a defensible line ΓÇö but it should be a stated one, since `discover_related`, `resolve_reference`, and `get_work` are in the same position.

5. **Was `download_to`'s guard set (`http.py:408ΓÇô422`) meant to be contract-tested, or is it defense-in-depth behind `composition.py`?** The empty-`allowed_hosts` case in particular disables host confinement outright, which reads like something worth pinning regardless.

6. **Does the compressed-and-truncated download case matter?** `http.py:503` skips the declared-length truncation check whenever `content-encoding` is present. I could not tell read-only whether providers in scope ever serve `content-encoding` on artifact routes.

7. **`tests/jsonl_engine/` was in scope for witnessing procurement behaviors, but I did not audit its 152 tests on their own terms** ΓÇö I read only the seven files reachable from the skip list and the materialization path. Say the word if the primary wants that tree censused too.
```

```
1	# agy — procurement redevelopment review (lanes A + B)
2	
3	**Date:** 2026-08-12 · **Mode:** read-only · **Agent:** agy (adjutant to the primary review agent)

7	---
8	
9	## Executive summary
10	
11	1. The redevelopment is **structurally sound**: every strong README claim I tested holds, and the `jsonl_engine` boundary is unusually clean — procurement re-implements nothing the engine owns.
12	2. Both of the primary's open questions resolve **in the implementation's favour**. The cancellation seam is safe: `acquire`, `import`, and `materialize` each carry their own `asyncio.shield`-and-settle wrapper, so no un-awaited thread can write after a reported cancellation.
13	3. The slug-traversal seam is likewise safe: the MCP `DepositSlug` regex *is* permissive as observed, but all seven client-supplied slug routes reach `validate_deposit_slug` before any filesystem touch. **No unguarded route exists.**
14	4. What survives from those two is a **contract-honesty** issue — the server advertises `PORTABLE_LEAF_PATTERN` and enforces something weaker — and **five separate copies** of the cancellation shield that have drifted apart.
15	5. The single largest real finding is duplication with **behavioral drift**: `_require_current` ×4, `_same_directory_generation` ×3, `_is_reparse` ×4 (two variants disagree on `S_ISLNK`), portable-leaf policy ×2, shield ×5.
16	6. One claim is **narrower than its prose**: redirect confinement and HTTPS-only are enforced in `download_to` only. `HttpClient.get` runs with `follow_redirects=True` and no host or scheme check — and OpenAlex sends its API key as a query parameter.
17	7. Test coverage is strong where it counts (extraction, materialization, acquisition) and thin at the edges: `download_to`'s five pre-flight guards, `RequestPolicy` validation, and redirect-count exhaustion have no tests at all.
18	8. **6 of 16 MCP tools are never invoked** by any test — including all three acquisition tools, the only reachable route to the `PLAN_ARTIFACT` capability.
19	9. The 10 reported skips are benign: 6 are symlink-privilege, and the one genuinely paired POSIX/Windows case (`test_documents.py:117`↔`:130`) is a model to copy.
20	10. **The more serious skip problem is invisible in the skip census**: four tests silently degrade to a happy-path assertion on Windows via `try/except OSError: pass`, so the invariants they name are never exercised here — yet they report as passed.
21	11. `test_materialization.py:510-515` already shows the correct pattern (branch on `os.name`, assert the Windows outcome). Three tests in the acquisition and local-import lanes should be converted to match.
22	12. Gap census is clean: **zero** TODO/FIXME/HACK/XXX markers in either tree, no unreachable provider capability, and no `None`-backed tool in the default composition.
23	13. Sci-Hub is declaration-only and the route for a DOI-only paper is local-import; that deferral is stated in README prose but has **no tracked brief**. Crossref's absence is entirely silent.
24	14. The PowerShell logistics lane is **complementary, not conflicting** — both lanes publish `article.json` and `inventory.jsonl` through the same `jsonl_engine` verbs. `latex-source-batch.ps1` holds one capability the Python lane lacks.
25	15. Highest-value follow-ups, in order: convert the four silently-degrading tests; decide the `DepositSlug` contract; confine or re-scope `HttpClient.get` redirects; consolidate the five shields; test `validate_deposit_slug` directly.
```

```
- **Cancellation seam** ΓÇö the premise about call shape is right, the conclusion doesn't follow. `acquire`, `import`, and `materialize` each carry their *own* `asyncio.shield`-and-settle wrapper (`operations/acquisition.py:109-125`, `local_import.py:146-155`, `materialization.py:112-123`), plus a fourth in the download worker (`http.py:122-145`). **Cross-lane verdict:** not conflicting. 698	**No form is advertised but unplannable — but `html` is terminal.** Once acquired and receipted it goes nowhere:
```
699	- `SourceMaterializationService._forms` (`materialization.py:142-152`) requires `source` (`:143-146`) and tolerates only an optional `pdf` (`:149-151`). An `html` form is silently ignored.
700	- `LocalImportService._classify` (`local_import.py:313-318`) accepts only `%PDF-` and gzip magic; **HTML cannot be locally imported at all** — an asymmetry between the two ingestion routes.
701	- `_FORM_ORDER` (`storage/acquisitions.py:25`) does include `"html": 2`, so it is a first-class receipt form.
702	
703	`html` is acquirable, receiptable, ordered — then inert. Whether that is staging for a future profile (parallel to the stated PDF-profile plan) or a dangling form is not settleable read-only. **The README never mentions `html` at all.** That silence is the reportable part.

708	
709	**R1 — `source/_safety.py` depth upgraded (lane A finding #7).** Lane A recorded it as via-service-only. Lane B's call-graph read refines this: `_portable_relative` / `_portable_leaf` **are** driven directly through `extraction.py:156` by `test_source_extraction.py:289` and `:336` (twelve invalid-path subtests plus four collision cases), and `_plain_directory` / `_regular_file` happy paths are driven through the unpinned entries at `extraction.py:278,280`, `latex.py:316`, and `tree.py:251`. **Only the reparse/symlink *rejection* branches (`_safety.py:75,94`) are unwitnessed**, and only because the tests that would drive them are the silently-skipped ones at `test_local_import.py:186-199`. Net: the module is better covered than lane A implied; the residual gap is narrower and sharper.
710	
711	**R2 — `domain/deposits.py` severity raised (lane A finding #11).** Lane A listed the untested `validate_deposit_slug` at position 11 of 13. Lane B §B1.9 shows it is the **single guard** stopping `..`, `CON`, and trailing-dot names on all seven client-supplied MCP slug routes, with eleven enforcement sites across five modules — and that the MCP layer above it deliberately does not enforce the equivalent. An untested guard carrying that much load belongs **above** the `download_to` pre-flight guards. Revised priority: this is the highest-value coverage gap in the review.
712	
713	**R3 — `finish_sync` cancellation gap narrowed but sharpened (lane A finding #6).** Lane A reported the MCP shield as an untested re-implementation of a capability-layer guarantee. So the untested one is `runtime.py` alone — and it is the variant that catches `except Exception` (`:20`) where `operations/acquisition.py:123` catches `except BaseException`. The gap is smaller than lane A implied and more specific: it is the one copy whose catch clause differs from its siblings.

715	**R4 — the six never-invoked MCP tools matter more than lane A said (finding #4).** Lane A noted the coverage gap neutrally. These are not speculative surface — they are the sole protocol path to the acquisition capability, and no test drives them end-to-end.

> **[thinking]**
>
> The `_is_reparse` latent bug claim is actually refuted—upstream guards already exist exactly where agy hypothesized they would. The stat operations in `acquisitions.py` pull from `root.stat_leaf()` which ultimately calls `os.stat()` with `follow_symlinks=False` in the jsonl_engine's publication module, preventing symlink traversal at the source.
>
> Since a POSIX symlink's lstat mode is `S_ISLNK`, the `stat.S_ISREG()` check fails before `_is_reparse` is even evaluated, and the attribute-only copies are only used at sites already paired with `S_ISREG`/`S_ISDIR` checks on lstat results. Beyond that, `open_leaf` adds `O_NOFOLLOW` and fstat device/inode cross-checks as additional defense. So while the four copies are inconsistent in shape—a real maintainability concern worth consolidating—there's no actual reachable hole. This downgrades the severity from latent bug to cosmetic drift with defense-in-depth intact.
>
> I've also confirmed the OpenAlex api_key exposure in the query string at those specific lines, and combined with the unconfined redirects, the recommendation to move it to configuration stands.
>
> Now I need to synthesize everything: append my triage addendum to the report, write a final chat summary with findings ranked and cost assessment, and update the memory files to reflect the current state of the Python procurement system and the probe-assisted triage pattern. The probe results show that test 755 exercises the real rename path when the parent is pinned, while test 802 keeps both subtests inert since the root rename is blocked. For the remaining questions, I'm recommending junction extension as a cheap win, at least one protocol-path test for plan/acquire using a mock provider, tightening the DepositSlug constraints to PORTABLE_LEAF_PATTERN, enforcing https-only in get() for non-loopback addresses, and refusing content-encoding headers on artifact downloads to make truncation checks universal. The guards for empty hosts already exist and just need better documentation, and contract tests for that are inexpensive to add.
>
> For the exception handling, I'm consolidating the shields to one helper while including the runtime's Exception→BaseException fix. The _is_reparse concern turns out not to be a bug since upstream guards like S_ISREG checks and O_NOFOLLOW opens already close the gap, so I'll keep the consolidation as a hygiene improvement and cite the relevant code. The html question aligns with the future profile reading, so a single README sentence should cover it. I'm recommending we mint new briefs for scihub-doi-access and crossref-resolver under issues/procurement/, and for the latex-source-batch question, keeping PS as the batch front-end for now since that's a destination question for the user. The PS-minted omit-freeze coupling should be documented as arguably correct, and for catalog_directory disclosure, the local MCP presents low risk so I'll recommend either aligning with inbox convention or accepting it deliberately.
>
> Now I need to write the addendum covering environment facts like temp poisoning and the fix suggestion, note that MCP registration is pending, flag the pycache residue, and list my verified items around transport, planning, and roots. Then I'll commit and follow with the final message.…

Review complete. The full report (agy lanes A+B plus my triage addendum) is committed at [agy-procurement-review-20260812.md](issues/procurement/discussions/agy-procurement-review-20260812.md) (`69d272e`).

## Verdict

The redevelopment is **structurally sound**. Every load-bearing README claim we tested holds in code: article.json publishes last (witnessed down to `mtime_ns`-level idempotence), destinations are genuinely no-replace, generation pinning runs through retained physical identities, plans never cross the MCP boundary, downloads are hop-checked/bounded mid-stream/hash-verified, and all seven client-supplied slug routes hit `validate_deposit_slug` before any filesystem touch. The jsonl_engine boundary is exceptionally clean — procurement re-implements nothing the engine owns, and the PS logistics lane is complementary (same engine verbs), not conflicting. Suite ground truth: **429 passed / 10 skipped**, 35s.

## What needs attention (ranked)

1. **Three tests are silently inert on Windows** — `test_acquisition.py:802` and `test_local_import.py:281/:186-199` degrade to happy-path asserts via `try/except OSError: pass` with no skip marker, so the pinning/symlink invariants they name never execute here yet report green. `test_materialization.py:510` shows the correct house pattern to convert them to. (My probe cleared the fourth suspect, `:755` — it runs its real branch.)
2. **`validate_deposit_slug` itself has no direct test** despite being the single guard carrying the whole slug story — because the MCP `DepositSlug` pattern advertises `PORTABLE_LEAF_PATTERN` but runtime-enforces a weaker regex that admits `..`/`CON`. No reachable traversal exists, but tighten the `StringConstraints` — it's free.
3. **`HttpClient.get` follows redirects unconfined** (`follow_redirects=True`, no scheme/host check) while the README reads as transport-wide — and OpenAlex sends its API key as a query parameter. Confine or re-scope. Related: `content-encoding` on an artifact response silently disables the truncation check; refusing it outright would close that.
4. **Five hand-rolled copies of the cancellation shield** (plus `_require_current` ×4, `_same_directory_generation` ×3, `_is_reparse` ×4, portable-leaf ×2) — all settle correctly, but they've drifted in shape (`Exception` vs `BaseException`, default vs dedicated executor). Consolidate.
5. **Six of 16 MCP tools are never invoked through the protocol** in tests — including all three acquisition tools, the *only* route to `PLAN_ARTIFACT`.
6. **Deferrals are untracked**: Sci-Hub (declaration-only) and Crossref (entirely silent) have no briefs — the three under `issues/procurement/` all describe the retired PS MCP. Also: `html` is acquirable and receipted but nothing consumes it, and the README never mentions it.

Integration notes: `scientiae-procurement` isn't registered in any `.mcp.json` yet (flagging as a checklist item, presumed deliberate); old-lane retirement is verified complete (no `_inbox`, no PS fetcher residue).

## Skepticism ledger

Two agy claims didn't survive verification: the `_is_reparse` "latent bug" is **refuted** (jsonl_engine's `follow_symlinks=False` stats + `O_NOFOLLOW` opens are the upstream guard it couldn't find), and "empty `allowed_hosts` disables confinement" was overstated (the guard exists at `http.py:413`, it's merely untested). Everything else I spot-checked cited exactly.

## Environment + para-agent test run

Two things you should know: **gemini CLI is not installed on this machine** (PATH, npm global, `~/.local/bin` all empty) — agy ran on headless claude instead, so budget accordingly: lane A $3.98, lane B session-cumulative $7.15. And **`C:\Users\azrie\AppData\Local\Temp\pytest-of-azrie` is ACL-poisoned** — bare `pytest` throws 273 phantom setup errors for anyone on this box until you delete/repair it (workaround baked into the report: `--basetemp`).

The para-agent mechanics themselves worked well: detach-launch (short `run` timeout, poll journal/deposit-file), `--resume` continuity produced a genuine cross-lane reconciliation section, and the deposit-file pattern beat journal fetching for the big payload. One tooling wart: `wait until:pattern` is unusable for headless turns since stale screen content matches. Lessons banked to memory.
